國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
內需與房市提供支撐 美國財政及通膨風險升高 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額為 7,450 億美元 ， 月增0.8%、 年增6.3%；
扣除汽車及零件銷售後月增 0.9%，進一步扣除汽車、零件及加油站後
之核心零售銷售月增 0.2%。加油站、餐飲及非實體零售銷售持續成長，
顯示美國消費需求仍具韌性，惟成長動能較前月略有放緩。 
⚫ 2026 財年美國聯邦政府財政赤字達 1 兆 9,924.7 億美元，年增 12.2%，
主因政府支出成長高於收入。9 月單月由去年同期盈餘轉為 269 億美元
赤字，關稅退稅增加壓縮收入，而全年淨利息支出突破1 兆美元、年增
8.4%。在聯邦債務總額逾 40 兆美元下，財政赤字擴大及債務融資成本
攀升，持續考驗財政永續性。 
⚫ 9 月美國成屋銷售年率 399 萬戶，月增 1.5%、年減 2.2%；成屋中位數
售價 41.75 萬美元，年增 1.3%，連續 39 個月上漲。待售庫存增至 160
萬戶 、 供給量4.8 個月 ， 顯示供給改善 ， 惟高利率環境仍抑制購屋需求 。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者物價指數 (CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.6%，高於 8 月之
0.4%，年增率由 3.4%升至 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，年增率由 2.4%
升至 2.6%。受能源價格上揚影響，能源指數月增 5.2%，其中汽油價格
大幅上漲，成為推升通膨之主要因素；住房指數續增0.4%。整體而言，
美國通膨壓力較前月升溫，核心物價漲勢亦略有增強。 
⚫ 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)月增 1.3%，年增率由 5.6%升至 6.3%；能
源價格大漲 10.8%為主要推升因素 ， 帶動商品價格月增2.6%。 服務價格
亦月增 0.8%，核心 PPI 年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%，顯示生產端通膨壓力
持續升高。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月中旬，多位聯準會官員表示通膨仍具壓力。Mary Daly 認為未來可
能仍需升息，但 10 月底會議無迫切行動必要；Beth Hammack 警告若通
膨預期惡化，可能加大緊縮力道；主席 Kevin Warsh 則指出美國經濟仍
具韌性，但能源價格上漲及 AI 投資擴張恐推升通膨壓力。整體而言，
聯準會立場偏鷹，但短期升息時點仍視通膨走勢而定。 
⚫ 美墨關稅談判原接近達成初步協議 ， 惟美國商務部長反對調降汽車及鋼
鋁關稅，使協商進展受阻。墨西哥則爭取降低汽車關稅及擴大零組件抵

短評 
⚫ 9 月美國零售銷售持續成長，顯示民間消費仍具韌性，房市交易亦略有

元 
區 
工業生產走疲，前景有待觀察 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)較 7 月下滑 0.5%， 呈現連續三個月下降。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐盟領袖峰會歐盟 27 個成員國普遍支持建立貿易的反制機制。該機制
將賦予歐盟在極端情況下 ， 迅速且全面封鎖特定國家企業進入歐洲單一
市場的權限。 
⚫ 歐中貿易談判公布相關細節 ， 未來中國出口至歐盟的混合動力汽車設定
每年 35 萬輛的配額上限(每年可遞增 6%)。 
短評 
⚫ 雖德國財政擴張有助支持工業生產，但法國政治、選舉等不利前景重挫
市場信心，恐會影響企業以及政府在各項支出、投資決策，進而形成拖
累因素。 
⚫ 歐盟的 「下一代歐盟復甦計畫」(NextGenerationEU)將在年底前完成所有
撥款，後續須關注是否有其他替代財政工具來緩解財政真空 (近期歐盟
領袖峰會雖有協商相關議題但無法取得共識)，以繼續支撐景氣動能。

本 
AI 投資續強，帶動機械訂單穩健復甦 
經濟 ⚫ 8 月核心機械訂單月增 5.2%。 
短評 
⚫ 8 月核心機械訂單優於市場預期，訂單成長已由科技業擴散至汽車及部
分服務業，顯示企業資本支出需求仍具韌性，第三季設備投資可望恢復
成長。

中 
國 
AI 相關需求帶動出口動能強勁成長 
經濟 
⚫ 9 月出口年增率較 8 月大幅增加 11.3 個百分點至 36.3%，出口總值為
4,472 億美元 。 進口年增率增2.8 個百分點至 31%， 進口總值為3,137 億
美元。 
⚫ 9 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 8 月的 3.8%升至 5%。 
⚫ 9 月消費者通膨(CPI)升 0.1 個百分點為 0.9%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 歐中貿易談判公布相關細節 ， 未來中國出口至歐盟的混合動力汽車設定
每年 35 萬輛的配額上限(每年可遞增 6%)。依照歐盟推算，中國對歐盟
的混合動力車出口量將較原先潛在規模減少一半以上。 
短評 
⚫ 雖歐中貿易協議不利於汽車出口 ， 但有助於避免歐盟進一步擴大貿易保
護措施，中國車廠則可能會加速透過赴歐投資設廠、提升當地生產比重
等方式因應配額限制，藉此維持在歐洲市場的競爭力與市占率。 
東 
(南) 
亞 
IMF 上調印度及東協的經濟展望；新、馬第三季 GDP 均逾 5% 
經濟 
⚫ IMF 上調印度及東協五國 2026 年 GDP 預估至 7.0%、4.4%。 
⚫ 新加坡 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率 5.4%。 
⚫ 馬來西亞 2026 年第三季經濟成長年增率 5.3%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 新加坡金融管理局 (MAS)10 月政策評估維持新元名目有效匯率政策區
間不變。 
短評 
⚫ IMF 指出印度內需及民間消費韌性強勁，而越南、馬來西亞、新加坡、
菲律賓及泰國為 AI 供應鏈受益國，但須留意地緣政治、全球貿易重組
及 AI 投資循環變化帶來的的通膨及經濟下行風險。 
台 
灣 
AI 帶動出口、投資與消費成長，中經院上修 2026 年經濟預測 
經濟 ⚫ 中經院 10 月 16 日公布最新預測，上修 2026 年台灣經濟成長率至12.16%。 
短評 ⚫ AI 需求持續擴張帶動出口與投資動能 ，惟地緣政治風險推升油價，後續通膨壓力仍值得關注。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.19 
一、 美國—內需與房市提供支撐 美國財政及通膨風險升高 
2026 年 9 月美國零售與餐飲服務銷售額經季節性調整後為 7,450 億美元，較
上月增加 0.8%，年增 6.3%。若扣除汽車及零件銷售，零售額月增 0.9%；進一步
扣除汽車、零件及加油站後，核心零售銷售月增0.2%，顯示整體消費需求仍維持
成長。從主要業別表現觀察，加油站、非實體零售商及餐飲服務與飲酒場所銷售
額均較上月增加，其中加油站及餐飲消費表現相對亮眼，電子及家電商店銷售亦
呈成長。整體而言，9 月美國零售銷售延續擴張走勢，顯示民間消費仍具支撐力
道，且多數主要消費類別維持正成長，惟核心零售增幅較前月放緩，後續仍須關
注物價、就業及金融情勢變化對消費支出的影響。 
2026 年 9 月(2026 財年第 12 個月)美國聯邦政府財政赤字為 269.0 億美元 ， 較
去年同期 1,979.4 億美元財政盈餘轉為赤字。當月政府收入 5,578.8 億美元，年增
2.6%；政府支出 5,847.8 億美元，年增 69.1%，支出增幅明顯高於收入。收入方
面，9 月關稅淨收入由 8 月的 128.4 億美元降至 59.1 億美元，累計2026 財年關稅
淨收入約 1,732.2 億美元，年減11.1%，關稅退稅增加已部分抵銷關稅收入成長。
支出方面，9 月聯邦政府淨利息支出約 347.9 億美元，全年累計達1 兆 517.5 億美
元 ， 年增8.4%， 反映高利率環境下債務利息負擔持續攀升 。 累計2026 財年全年 ，
美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1 兆 9,924.7 億美元，較 2025 財年擴大 12.2%。其中，
政府收入年增 3.2%至 5 兆 4,033.3 億美元 ， 支出年增5.5%至 7 兆 3,958.0 億美元 ，
顯示支出成長仍快於收入增加。從支出結構觀察，社會安全、醫療保險、國防及
淨利息支出均較上年增加，持續推升財政壓力。整體而言，美國財政赤字擴大主
要反映政府支出成長強勁、利息負擔居高不下，以及關稅退稅增加削弱部分收入
效果 。 在聯邦債務總額突破40 兆美元背景下 ， 未來財政赤字走勢 、 債務融資成本
及財政永續性問題，仍將是市場關注的重要焦點。 
2026 年 9 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率升至 399 萬戶 ， 較上月增加1.5%，
惟較去年同期減少 2.2%，整體房市成交動能仍偏疲弱。區域表現方面，南部及西
部分別月增 2.8%，中西部持平，東北部月減 2.1%，四大地區銷售量均較去年同
期下滑。房價方面，成屋售價中位數為 41.75 萬美元，年增 1.3%，已連續 39 個

月維持年增走勢。供給端則持續增加，待售成屋庫存升至 160 萬戶，較上月增加
1.9%， 較去年同期增加4.6%， 相當於4.8 個月供給量 ， 高於去年同期的4.5 個月 。
全美住房可負擔指數由去年同期 106.7 降至 105.4， 顯示購屋負擔仍受房價及利率
影響。整體而言，9 月美國成屋市場呈現成交量略有回升、房價維持溫和漲勢及
待售庫存持續累積的格局，顯示供給條件改善，但高利率環境仍對購屋需求形成
壓力。 
2026 年 9 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.6%，高於上月之
0.4%，年增率由 3.4%升至 3.7%；扣除食品與能源之核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，與上月
持平，年增率則由 2.4%升至 2.6%。在細項方面，能源價格持續上揚，能源指數
月增 5.2%，其中汽油價格大幅上漲 9.1%，貢獻整體 CPI 月增幅逾半，為本月通
膨升溫主因；住房指數月增 0.4%，住房項目價格漲幅尚未明顯趨緩。食品指數則
持平，其中外食價格月增0.2%，家庭食品價格月減 0.1%。此外，二手車及卡車、
機票、休閒娛樂及服飾等價格上漲，通訊價格則下跌。整體而言，9 月美國通膨
明顯回升，主要受汽油價格大幅上漲及住房成本續增帶動；核心 CPI 月增率維持
0.3%，年增率則回升至 2.6%，顯示核心物價壓力較前月略有增強，未來通膨走勢
仍須持續關注能源價格變化及住房等服務項目價格表現。 
 2026 年 9 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 1.3%，高於上月之
0.5%，年增率則由 5.6%升至 6.3%。其中，最終需求商品價格月增 2.6%，主要受
能源價格月增 10.8%帶動，以汽油、柴油及航空燃油價格漲幅較為顯著；食品價
格亦月增 1.2%。另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.8%，主要反映批發及零售
利潤率、運輸服務價格上升。剔除食品、能源及貿易服務後之核心最終需求 PPI
月增 0.5%，高於上月之 0.3%，年增率由 4.8%升至 5.0%。整體而言，9 月 PPI 升
幅明顯擴大，主要反映能源與服務價格同步上揚，顯示生產端成本壓力仍偏高，
後續仍須關注能源價格及運輸成本變化對通膨走勢之影響。 
10 月中旬，多位聯準會官員針對貨幣政策發表看法。舊金山聯準銀行總裁戴
利(Mary Daly) 表示，雖然通膨仍高於 2%目標，未來可能仍需進一步升息，但目
前無迫切必要在 10 月 27 日至 28 日 FOMC 會議立即採取行動，顯示聯準會傾向
先觀察後續數據表現。克里夫蘭聯準銀行總裁哈瑪克(Beth Hammack)則警告，若
通膨預期持續惡化，聯準會可能必須採取更積極的緊縮措施，以維持物價穩定。
聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)10 月 16 日則表示，美國經濟受穩健就業市場及 AI

投資熱潮支撐而維持韌性，但能源價格上漲及 AI 投資擴張恐推升通膨壓力，並
未對下次利率決策預作表態 。 整體而言 ， 聯準會官員發言顯示政策立場仍偏鷹派 ，
但對短期升息時點保持彈性，未來通膨及通膨預期變化仍是利率決策的重要依據。 
近期美墨貿易談判再度受到關稅爭議影響。據《華爾街日報》報導，雙方原
已接近達成初步協議 ， 美國擬調降對墨西哥汽車 、 鋼鐵及鋁製品的部分進口關稅 ，
以換取墨西哥在貿易政策、商品採購及其他經貿議題上的讓步，並為後續《美墨
加協定》 （USMCA） 檢討與延長協商創造有利條件 。 然而 ， 美國商務部長Howard 
Lutnick 反對大幅調降目前對墨西哥汽車課徵的 25%關稅 ， 以及鋼鐵 、 鋁製品與部
分衍生製成品的 50%關稅，使談判進展受阻。墨西哥方面則希望將汽車關稅降至
15%或以下，以縮小與日本、南韓汽車適用稅率的差距，並擴大汽車關稅抵減機
制對美墨加三國零組件價值的認列範圍；惟美方仍須考量國內金屬產業對降稅可
能衝擊本土生產及就業的疑慮。儘管雙方對關稅調整幅度仍存分歧，墨西哥經貿
官員表示協商已有部分進展，美國商務部亦強調持續與墨方保持溝通，顯示談判
尚未正式破局。整體而言，美墨關稅協商結果不僅攸關北美汽車及金屬產業供應
鏈成本與競爭力，也可能影響 USMCA 後續檢討與延長協商進程；若雙方遲遲無
法縮小分歧，恐增加企業投資與供應鏈布局的不確定性，並對北美區域貿易整合
形成壓力。 
二、歐元區—工業生產走疲，前景有待觀察 
 歐元區 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)較 7 月下滑 0.5%，呈現連續三個月下降，
其中非耐久消費財、中間財及能源產出分別減少 2.7%、0.8%、0.6%，僅資本財、
耐久消費財產出分別成長 0.4%、0.2%。儘管德國 8 月工業生產(不含營造業)反彈
0.4%，係受惠車廠陸續結束歲修，但法、義、西工業生產分別下跌0.3%、1.3%、
0.8%為主要拖累因素，反映先前美伊戰爭帶來的庫存儲備需求逐漸消退，尤其以
能源密集產業(化工、造紙、 鋼鐵等)變化最為明顯。整體而言，即便排除能源密集
產業的波動，其他製造業諸如製藥、汽車、機械等生產情形皆偏疲軟。 
 展望未來，近期德國在財政擴張的支持下，工廠訂單大幅成長，未來可望陸
續反映在該國工業生產表現；但法國政治、選舉等不利前景重挫市場信心，恐會
影響企業以及政府在各項支出 、 投資決策 ， 進而形成拖累因素 。 另外 ， 歐盟的 「下

四、中國—AI 相關需求帶動出口動能強勁成長

億美元 ； 累計前九個月出口年增率上升為21.3%， 累積出口總值為33,707 億美元 。
前九個月出口主要成長項目(累積年增率)包含 IC(108.1%)、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等，54.6%)、船舶(29.9%)、汽車(54.3%)，其中 IC 及自動數據處理設備
出口加速成長，為 9 月出口年增率大幅上升的主要因素。 
9 月進口年增率較 8 月增 2.8 個百分點至 31%，進口總值為 3,137 億美元；累
計前九個月進口值成長 27.5%至 24,326 億美元。其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包
含伺服器等)進口分別成長 63.5%、124.8%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部
分伺服器關鍵組件有賴於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前九個月貿易順差達
9,381 億美元，僅較去年同期增加 7.7%，增幅明顯低於出口，顯示出口擴張同時
伴隨關鍵原物料進口大幅增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價
值產品依賴國際供應鏈支持。

六、台灣—AI 帶動出口、投資與消費成長，中經院上修 2026 年經濟預測 
中經院 10 月 16 日公布最新預測，上修 2026 年台灣經濟成長率至 12.16%，
創 1988 年以來新高，主要受惠於 AI 技術快速發展帶動全球科技投資，半導體與
資通訊產品需求強勁，出口維持高速成長，同時民間投資與消費亦同步擴張，經
濟成長動能由內、外需共同支撐。另一方面，由於中東地緣政治風險升溫帶動油
價維持高檔，能源價格外溢效果推升整體物價，中經院將 2026 年 CPI 年增率預
估上修至 2.06%，惟整體通膨仍屬相對溫和。

Summary: We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show that 
was held on 20-22 October. Below, we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key 
company takeaways. Overall, we remain positive on Taiwan glass fibre, CCL, PCB, and 
substrate vendors, supported by upbeat company commentary. On pricing, we expect 
low-end CCL and E-glass price increases to be larger and more sustained than previously 
anticipated. High-end CCL vendor commentary suggests plans for more meaningful price 
increases from 2H26 to maintain their products’ relative profitability amid sharp increases 
in low-end CCL prices. We also expect prices for T-glass, ABF, CCL, and substrates to 
continue rising. On the CCL technology roadmap, we favour Q-glass combined with 
hydrocarbon resin (HC) over PTFE-based alternatives as the longer-term direction. Lastly, 
we provide our view on the CCL market competition landscape change after seeing 
Nan Ya Plastics targeting the high-end market more aggressively. We also provide some of 
our key highlights from related companies within the Taiwan PCB supply chain. EMC (2383 
TT, TWD4,750, Buy [1]), ITEQ (6213 TT, TWD746, Buy [1]), Kinsus (3189 TT, TWD941, 
Buy [1]) and NYPCB (8046 TT, TWD1,255, Buy [1]) are our top ideas within our coverage.

We attended the 2026 Taiwan Printed Circuit Association (TPCA) Show last week. Below, 
we share our read-throughs from booth visits and key company takeaways.

Whether to adopt PTFE emerged as a key point of debate at the show.  
• During the TPCA show, Taiwan Union Technology (TUC; 6274 TT, TWD1,280, Buy [1]) 
unveiled its new M10 (Hydrocarbon base) and M11 (PTFE base) materials with glass-
fabric (Low-Dk2, Q-glass) and glass-fabric-free versions. Nan Ya Plastic (1303 TT, not 
rated) also unveiled its M11-grade material (ie, NPG-A99) at the show. Based on our 
understanding, NPG-A99 will continue to a hydrocarbon resin (HC) base, likely 
combined with Low-Dk2, to enhance CCL performance for high frequency and high-
speed signal transmission requirements. However, Elite Material (EMC; 2383 TT, 
TWD4,750, Buy [1]) remains reluctant to enter the PTFE-based CCL market, citing 
limited end application. Also, EMC plans to use advanced Q-glass for its M10 CCL. 
Lastly, we also heard from Panasonic’s booth representative that Panasonic (6752 JP, 
JPY3,687, Neutral [3]; covered by Ayaka Inomata) is pursuing a similar strategy for 
M9/M10 development, leveraging Q-glass instead of PTFE at this stage.

• While Nvidia (NVDA US, USD216, Outperform [2]; covered by Louis Miscioscia) 
appears keen to enhance product performance by adopting PTFE, given its very low 
dissipation factor (Df), PCB manufacturing yields remain a key concern. Accordingly, 
some leading vendors are broadening their material options (including PTFE-based 
CCL) to address potential customer requirements. EMC and Panasonic, however, 
remain firmly committed to a Q-glass-based technology roadmap. Although TUC 
highlighted progress on its PTFE-based M11 at a technology conference during the 
show, we believe it also intends to advance its existing HC resin/Low-Dk2 and HC 
resin/Q-glass roadmap for its next-generation CCL. In our view, its PTFE-based M11 
development is intended to preserve flexibility across potential technology pathways 
rather than replace its existing approach. Nan Ya Plastics’s strategy also appears 
reasonable: it can produce Low-Dk glass in-house but must source Q-glass and PTFE 
externally. This sourcing structure may explain its continued use of Low-Dk2 with HC-
resin instead of PTFE for its next-gen products, including M11.

MEMO 
 Taiwan PCB: Our key takeaways from 2026 TPCA show

Rating: Positive
Sheng Cheng
(886) 2 8758 6253
sheng.cheng@daiwacm-cathay.com.tw
Thomas Kim
(886) 2 8758 6249
thomas.kim@daiwacm-cathay.com.tw

### Page 2

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware 
27 October 2026 
2

• Conclusion: which technology roadmap will prevail? We believe it is too early to 
identify a clear winner, as the supply chain continues to improve manufacturing yields 
for PTFE-based products. Some vendors remain cautious despite PTFE’s long history in 
the industry, as its thermal shrinkage and relative softness still make adoption in high-
layer-count (HLC) PCBs challenging. Given the current stance of leading global vendors 
EMC, Doosan (000150 KS), and Panasonic, together with EMC’s execution track 
record, we currently favour Q-glass-based solutions without PTFE as the more likely 
direction. Nevertheless, broadening product offerings to address different customer 
requirements should not be viewed negatively, provided vendors have sufficient 
resources to support development across multiple technology pathways.

Low-end CCL and e-glass continue to surprise on the upside.  
• During the show, we also spoke with Kingboard Laminates (1888 HK, not rated). The 
company remained positive on the E-glass outlook, although its booth representative 
provided no numerical guidance for next year following a c.200% price hike in 2026. 
Kingboard attributed the pricing strength to supply constraints, driven largely by AI 
server demand. While we highlighted increased glass yarn supply from Chinese peers in 
2Q/3Q26, the company argued that the weaving machine capacity remains the 
bottleneck. It expects to add only c.500 weaving machines in 2026 and 1.2k in 2027 (vs. 
current installed base of 3.3k). E-glass fabric price increases therefore appear 
supported by limited weaving capacity and producers shifting capacity from lower-end 
glass to higher-end products, including Low-Dk, Low-Dk2, and T-glass. Kingboard also 
noted a similar trend in low-end copper foil, where shortages have recently intensified.

• The read-through from Nan Ya Plastics is similar. The company indicated that its current 
fibreglass allocation between internal consumption and external sales is 45:55, with the 
internal share set to rise gradually as it upgrades its product mix. Its CCL portfolio 
remains concentrated in FR-4 (M2 grade), but it plans to migrate toward M4/M6, 
supported by long-term contract wins with two major hyperscalers seeking to secure 
CCL supply. In 2027, M4/M6 products are expected to account for 60-70% of its mix (4m 
out of 6.5m in capacity), with M2 and below contributing 20%. The remainder would 
comprise high-end CCL, including M7/M8/M9, reflecting recent development progress. 
We therefore expect Nan Ya’s external thin-type E-glass supply to decline as its M4/M6 
mix increases next year.

Competition from Nan Ya Plastics.   
• The current CCL shortage creates favourable conditions for Nan Ya Plastics to expand 
into the high-end CCL supply chain. Whether the company has entered AMZN’s T3/T4 
supply chain is also a key point of market debate. Based on discussions with its booth 
representatives, however, we believe management will prioritise upgrading its M4/M6 
product portfolio before pursuing the highest-end CCL market more aggressively. On the 
positive side, favourable supply-demand dynamics have supported Nan Ya’s transition 
from a low-end supplier toward the mid-to-high-end segment, although high-end CCL 
should still contribute a relatively small share of revenue in 2027. For incumbent 
suppliers, competition is clearly intensifying, but we expect the near-term impact on 
high-end peers to remain limited.

• Our read: We expect the impact on EMC and TUC to remain relatively limited in 2027. 
Competitive pressure could become more meaningful over 2028-2030, although it is too 
early to assess its magnitude.

### Page 3

Taiwan Data Centre Hardware 
27 October 2026 
3

Other key takeaways: 
• Nitto Boseki (3110 JP, JPY2,942, Neutral [3]; covered by Noritsugu Hirakawa) identified 
Taiwan Glass (1802 TT, NR) and Grace Fabric Technology (603256 CH, CNY84.95, 
Outperform [2]; covered by Dennis Ip) as key competitors. Its plan to triple capacity by 
2028 remains largely on track. The company also intends to expand capacity further by 
2030, although the details have yet to be confirmed.

• Resonac (4004 JP, JPY12,915, Outperform [2]; covered by Hidemitsu Umebayashi) 
indicated that customers are requesting ABF substrate core thicknesses of 2,000-
3,000μm, versus its current maximum of 1,600μm, to accommodate larger package 
sizes. Potential technology pathways include adopting glass cores or ceramic 
substrates, or stacking additional core layers to enhance rigidity. Based on discussions 
with booth representatives, substrate makers may move toward multi-core structures, 
which could increase T-glass consumption. However, technical challenges in enabling 
signal transmission between core layers remain a potential barrier that would need to be 
addressed over the next one to two years.

• Mitsui Kinzoku (5706 JP, JPY2,054, Outperform [2]; covered by Shinichiro Ozaki) 
indicated that, based on its technology roadmap, M10-grade CCL combined with its 
HVLP5 copper foil should support 448Gbps SerDes transmission. In the near term, the 
company plans to shift capacity from lower-end to higher-end HVLP products, with a key 
piece of production equipment scheduled for installation in 2028/29. On pricing, its booth 
representative suggested that quarterly increases of more than 10% would be 
appropriate. Regarding copper foil shortages highlighted by other supply-chain 
participants, the representative indicated that mid-tier products, such as HVLP1 and 
HVLP2, could face the most severe supply constraints.

• Unimicron (3037 TT, TWD1,060, Buy [1]) appeared to highlight multi-core structures and 
the embedding of passive components within core layers as key technology trends in 
ABF substrates.

• Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT; 4958 TT, not rated) indicated that its substrate business 
has a plan to list on the Hong Kong Stock Exchange in 1H27 under the name Leading 
Interconnect Semiconductor Technology (LIST). At the show, the company focused 
primarily on thick HDI boards and highlighted two next-generation technology trends: 1) 
sequential lamination (M+N) to improve signal integrity and increase routing density 
within the same footprint; and 2) hybrid HDI/HLC structures, which add HDI layers to 
thick HLC boards (M+N), as discussed earlier.

煉油利差仍佳，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 上修 4.2%至 2.04 元 
煉油利差亮麗，3Q26 營業利益 320.82 億元，年增 245.6%，稅後 EPS 為
2.76 元，年增 206.3%，獲利創單季新高。4Q26 冬季取暖維繫柴油消費動
能抵銷煉油開工滑落，預估 4Q26營收維持 1939.85億元，季減 20.4%，年
增 34.9%，營益率上修 0.5ppt至 11.9%，營業利益上修 4.4%至 230.55億
元，季減 28.1%，年增 338.4%，稅後 EPS 上修 4.2%至 2.04 元，季減
26.1%，年增 280.3%，4Q26營益率季減係反映汽油消費轉淡，煉油利差收
斂，惟沙烏地 OSP 折價擴大至 5 美元，汽油價差優於預期，因而上修獲利。 
預估 1Q27 稅後 EPS 上修 3.5%至 1.78 元 
展望 1Q27 農曆年假工作天數較少，不過受損的煉油產能難以恢復，冬天取
暖用油需求仍強，柴油供需持續偏緊，維繫價差高檔，預估 1Q27 營收維持
1833.16億元，季減 5.5%，年增 13.2%，營益率上修 0.3ppt至 10.7%，營
業利益上修 2.9%至 196.66億元，季減 14.7%，年減 19.8%，稅後 EPS上
修 3.5%至 1.78元，季減 12.9%，年減 17.0%。獲利年減，乃因美伊戰爭爆
發導致 1Q26基期較高，不過 1Q27獲利仍遠優於 2025年各季表現。 
煉油產能受損難以恢復，供應吃緊，利差展望佳 
煉油產能受損難以恢復，供給吃緊，估 2026 年營收上修 1.1%至 7826.12
億元，年增 25%，稅後 EPS上修 1.2%至 9.12元，年增 780.1%，推估配
息 6.39元，殖利率達 5.2%以上，仍具吸引力。預期 2027年營收 7559.99
億元，年減 3.4%，加計南亞科現金股利挹注，稅後 EPS上修 10.9%至 6.84
元，雖年減 25.1%，遠優於 2025年獲利低迷狀態。目前本淨比 2.17倍，處
於歷史本淨比 1.0倍-3.0倍區間中間，重申買進建議，目標價由 125元上調
至 160元，本淨比由 2.2倍上修至 2.8倍及 2027年預估每股淨值 56.36元
推得，本淨比上修係反映煉油利差亮麗，提振獲利，評價上修。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 稅後 EPS 2.76 元，年增 206.3%，創單季新高。而煉油利差仍
佳，預估 4Q26/2026 稅後 EPS 上修 4.2%/1.2%至 2.04 元/9.12 元。 
⧫  
柴油價差維持高檔，且加計南亞科股利收入挹注， 預估 1Q27/2027 年
稅後 EPS 各上修 3.5%/10.9%至 1.78 元/6.84 元。 
⧫  
煉油產能受損 ，成品油供應吃緊， 提振利差，重申買進，目標價 上調
至 160 元，基於 2.8 倍本淨比及 2027 年估每股淨值 56.36 元推得。

營運分析
煉油利差亮麗，台塑化 3Q26 稅後 EPS 續增至 2.76 元，季增 26.5%，年增
206.3%，優於預期 1.1%
台塑化(6505)公布 3Q26自結獲利續揚，動能亮麗。3Q26營收 2436.54億元(原估 2351.33億元)，
季增 33.2%，年增 49%，優於預期 3.6%，儘管成品油均價季跌 4.7%至 128.9美元，小幅回檔，
不過原油供應恢復，開工率季增 24.7ppt至 91.2%，成品油銷售量季增 43.7%至 4832.3萬桶，帶
動煉油營收季增 35%，至於烯烴事業營收季增 17%，乃係原料供應增加，開工率季增 6.5ppt 至
39.6%，銷售量季增 26.1%至 78.3 萬噸，至於公用事業營收季增 12.3%，反映夏季電費較高，及
售電量與售氣量均增溫。
3Q26營益率季增 0.7ppt至 13.17%(原估 13.19%)，符合預期，營業利益 320.82億元(原估 310.15

億元)，季增 40.2%，年增 245.6%，優於預期 3.4%，業外現金股利收入約 6.4億元，稅後 EPS為
2.76元(原估 2.73元)，季增 26.5%，年增 206.3%，優於預期 1.1%，獲利創單季新高。3Q26營益
率回升係因美伊談判陷入膠著，中東煉油產能恢復延後，而俄羅斯煉油廠遭受烏克蘭攻擊，產出下
滑，煉油利差自 6 月低點逐步走高，加上沙烏地阿拉伯調降對亞洲原油的官方售價(OSP)，成本下
降，提振 3Q26煉油利差擴大，加上認列 27.9億元存貨評價利益，帶動整體獲利走高。

汽油消費轉淡且歲修干擾，惟整體煉油利差仍優於預期，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 上
修 4.2%至 2.04 元，年增 280.3% 
展望 4Q26 美伊談判陷入膠著，難以擺脫僵局狀態，且沙烏地與葉門胡塞組織戰火擴大，預期中東
局勢仍將處於不穩定狀態，油價延續高檔整理，而原油供應應可處於穩定狀態，且歐洲積極建立柴
油庫存，以滿足冬季取暖需求，有利柴油價格強勢。另一方面，儘管汽油消費進入淡季，不過煉油
廠歲修較為密集，開工率降低，且全球汽油庫存偏低，及中國限制成品油出口，帶動汽油價格 於
10 月回彈，汽油價差優於預期，而煉油基礎單元於 10 月至 11 月進行歲修，煉油開工率由 3Q26
的 91%降至 75%，產銷量下滑，至於烯烴事業營運平淡，預估開工率維持在 35-40%左右。維持
4Q26營收 1939.85億元之預估，季減 20.4%，年增 34.9%，營益率上修 0.5ppt至 11.9%%(原估
11.4%)，營業利益上修 4.4%至 230.55億元(原估 220.85億元)，季減 28.1%，年增 338.4%，稅後
EPS上修 4.2%至 2.04元(原估 1.96元)，季減 26.1%，年增 280.3%，4Q26營益率季減係反映歲
修干擾，稼動率下滑之影響，惟沙烏地阿拉伯對亞洲原油的官方售價(OSP)折價幅度擴大至 5 美元，
成本下降，整體獲利仍優於原先預期，台塑化 4Q26獲利表現，將遠優於 4Q25同期。

預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 上修 1.2%至 9.12 元，年增 780.1% 
展望 2026 年，美伊戰爭引發的中東局勢動盪可能往常態化發展，中東產能運作受衝擊，且俄羅斯
煉油廠遭受烏克蘭攻擊，產出下滑，而歐美老舊煉油廠相繼關廠退出市場，中國亦有淘汰老舊煉油
廠產能規劃，供給受限，而美國恐限制柴油出口，成品油供給吃緊，而成品油需求穩健，維繫煉油
利差。台塑化獲利回溫，預估 2026 年營收上修 1.1%至 7826.12 億元(原估 7740.90 億元)，年增
25%，營益率上修 0.1ppt 至 13.1%(原估 13.0%)，營業利益上修 2.0%至 1025.35 億元(原估
1004.97億元)，年增 842.5%，稅後 EPS上修 1.2%至 9.12元(原估 9.01元)，年增 780.1%，年底
淨值 55.91元(原估 55.80元)。

預估 1Q27 稅後 EPS 上修 3.5%至 1.78 元，年減 17%  
展望 1Q27 農曆年假工作天數較少，成品油產出下滑，不過美伊和談進展不順，中東政局動盪，預
期油價延續高檔格局，中東及俄羅斯煉油產能受損情況則難以快速恢復，柴油供給受限，至於需求
端，冬天取暖用油需求仍強，預期柴油供需仍將延續緊俏短缺格局。有利柴油價格及價差延續強勢，
預估 1Q27營收維持 1833.16億元，季減 5.5%，年增 13.2%，營益率上修 0.3ppt至 10.7%(原估
10.4%)，營業利益上修 2.9%至 196.66億元(原估 191.16億元)，季減 14.7%，年減 19.8%，稅後
EPS上修 3.5%至 1.78元(原估 1.72元)，季減 12.9%，年減 17.0%。1Q27獲利年減，乃因美伊戰
爭爆發，推動 2026 年 3 月之後成品油價格飆漲，價差擴大，墊高基期所致，不過獲利仍遠優於
2025年各季表現。

預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 上修 10.9%至 6.84 元，年減 25.1% 
展望 2027 年，OPEC+增產原油恐壓抑油價，不過中東局勢動盪常態化，加上 OECD 國家回補原
油庫存，將支撐油價，預期油價延續偏高檔整理，惟預期中東及俄羅斯受損的煉油產能可能逐步恢
復運作，煉油利差將較 2026 年滑落，但仍會優於美伊戰爭前的水準，預估 2027 年營收維持
7559.99億元，年減 3.4%，營益率上修 0.1ppt至 9.1%(原估 9.0%)，年減 4.0ppt，營業利益上修
0.8%至 684.10 億元(原估 678.60 億元)，年減 33.3%，業外方面，加計持有南亞(1303)及南亞科
(2408)預期將配發之現金股利合計推估約 72.7億元之挹注，稅後 EPS上修 10.9%至 6.84元(原估
6.17元)，年減 25.1%，年底淨值 56.36元(原估 55.66元)。 
若以 70%股利配發率計算，推估 2027年台塑化將配息 6.39元，以目前股價計算，殖利率達 5.2%
以上，仍具吸引力。

中東煉油產能受損及俄羅斯煉油廠受攻擊，維繫煉油價差表現，提振台塑化獲利 
煉油事業營運方面，2026 年 2 月底美伊戰爭爆發，驅動油價大漲，成品油價格也隨之走高，其中
柴油報價自 2月底約 90美元一路走高至 4月上旬的 220美元以上，柴油價差也自 14美元擴大至
140美元，汽油報價則自 2月底約 77美元一路走高至 3月下旬 160美元以上，汽油價差自 1美元
擴大至 48 美元，隨著美伊於 4月啟動和談，油價處於高檔震盪，成品油價格也同樣處於高位，柴
油價差則自 4月初高點收斂至 50-60美元區間，汽油價差則收斂至 20-30美元區間，2026/6/12美
伊雙方針對停火條件達成共識，並於 6/17正式簽署包含 14個要點諒解備忘錄 MOU，油價及成品
油價格見到進一步修正，柴油價差收斂至 30-35美元區間，汽油價差收斂至 15-20美元。 
7月以來美伊戰爭再起，油價大漲，成品價格隨之走高，價差再度擴大，柴油價差擴大至 50-60美
元，汽油價差擴大至 25-30美元，8月及 9月柴油價差擴大至 60-70美元，10月持續維持在 60美
元以上，汽油價差於 8月及 9月維持在 20-25美元之間，10月則突破 30美元以上，優於預期，汽
油價差擴大乃因全球汽油產能歲修較為密集，開工率降低，且全球汽油庫存偏低，以及中國限制成
品油出口，帶動汽油價格回彈。儘管柴油及汽油價差自 4月初的高點收斂，不過對比美伊戰前，柴
油價差多處於 15-25美元區間，汽油價差多處於 10-15美元，近期柴油及汽油價差仍高於美伊戰前
水準。另一方面，基於 OPEC+決議原油增產，市場競爭加劇，沙烏地阿拉伯則調降對亞洲原油的
官方售價(OSP)，6月為溢價 15.5美元，7月為溢價 9.5美元，8月降至折價 1.5美元，9月折價
2.0美元，10月延續折價 2.0美元，11月折價幅度再擴大至 5.0美元，推估 12月仍將維持折價狀
態，亞洲國家原油採購成本降低，可維繫煉油利差處於高檔水準。展望後市，由於中東煉油設備受
損，成品油產能仍須一段時間才有機會逐漸恢復，且俄羅斯煉油廠遭受烏克蘭攻擊，產出下滑，成
品油供給相對偏緊，美國川普總統施壓歐洲國家釋出柴油庫存，顯示供需缺口擴大，預期柴油及汽
油價差可維持高於美伊戰前水準，有利台塑化煉油事業獲利維持亮麗表現，較 2025年大幅改善。

南亞科(2408)股價波動劇烈，先保守以 2Q26 底收盤價作為評價基礎，推估台塑化
2026 年底淨值 55.91 元 
台塑化藉由”透過其他綜合損益按公允價值衡量之金融資產 -非流動”會計科目持有南亞科(2408)股權
約 10.81%，每季底依據南亞科股價變動造成的持股市值變化將反映在台塑化之股權權益及淨值。
台塑化分別於 2025/10/28、11/11及 12/31公告相繼已處分 2.5萬、1.8萬及約 1.9萬張南亞科持
股，平均處分價格各為 98.67元、145.09元及 169.46元，台塑化目前持有南亞科股權降至 8.81%。
簡單試算，若南亞科股價每變動 10元，將造成台塑化淨值變動約 0.29元。 
若以南亞科最新 (2026/10/17) 股價 447.5元計算，對比 2026/6/30收盤價 452.5元，台塑化持股
8.81%的評價利益將較 2Q26底減少約 13.64億元，將導致台塑化淨值減少約 0.14元。由於南亞科
股價波動較為劇烈，先保守以 2026/6/30 收盤價作為評價基礎，推估台塑化 2026 年底淨值為
55.91元(原估 55.80元) ，2026年淨值大幅提升乃係反映持股南亞科股價大漲後的評價利益貢獻。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 10月 19日 FPCC 第7 頁，共 18頁  
產業概況  
美伊和談進展反覆，油價於 6 月大幅下挫超過 30%後，3Q26 大幅反彈 
美國與伊朗於 2026年 4月初開始啟動停火協議談判，經歷過 2個多月斡旋及拉鋸，於 2026/6/12
雙方針對停火條件達成共識，並於 6/17正式簽署包含 14個要點諒解備忘錄 MOU，承諾在 60天內
達成最終全面協議。在美伊進行停火談判期間，荷姆茲海峽仍處於封鎖狀態，全球原油供應仍有短
缺，不過 OECD 主要國家以原油庫存支應需求，加上美伊和談的預期心理，油價即面臨震盪拉回
的修正壓力，期間美伊談判反覆幾度再推動油價反彈，但都未能突破 3月高點，呈現高檔整理格局，
美伊雙方談判一度進展順利，油價於 5月初高點 100美元以上，一路修正至 6月下旬波段低點，西
德州原油跌破 70 美元，布蘭特原油跌破 75 美元，波段跌幅均超過 30%，走勢疲弱，已跌至
2026/2/28 美伊戰爭爆發前的油價區間 65-70 美元。3Q26 以來，美伊談判陷入反覆，並再次交火，
油價再反彈回升至 100美元以上。近期美伊戰火停息，美國計畫改採對伊朗經濟與貿易制裁手段，
美伊雙方對和談條件的歧見仍深，短期難以達成和談共識，談判陷入膠著，油價將呈現高檔震盪格
局。 
圖 6：西德州與布蘭特原油價格走勢圖(美元/桶)

資料來源：Platts、元大投顧整理

美國釋出戰備庫存壓制油價，亦可望於低檔回補庫存，預期油價上檔壓力區 100-
110 美元，下檔支撐區 70-75 美元 
另一方面，觀察 OECD 國家原油庫存趨勢，美伊戰爭開打後，各國政府為因應荷姆茲海峽封鎖造
成的原油供應短缺，先提供原油庫存滿足原油需求，造成原油庫存於近 4 個月庫存大減 2.49 億桶
至 25.59億桶，為 2024年 2月以來新低，也低於過去 4年平均的 27.7億桶。3Q26油價一度大漲，
為壓制油價漲勢，美國能源部宣布將從戰略石油儲備釋出 4000 萬桶原油，並敦促歐洲成員國釋出
戰備原油儲備，預期 OECD 國家原油庫存恐延續滑落格局。荷姆茲海峽仍有油輪通行，加上替代
油路，中東產油國原油運輸恢復，供應趨於穩定，亦有機會壓制原油價格漲勢，預期油價 100-110
美元區間將是上檔壓力區。至於油價的支撐區研判落在 70-75美元，乃考量各國政府有動機於油價
相對低檔之際，進行原油庫存回補，可為油價形成支撐。

全球煉油產能受損比率約 6.7%，成品油供應受衝擊 
全球煉油產能約 1.03億桶，主要煉油產能集中在亞太地區占比 38.3%最大，其次為北美 20.9%，
歐洲 11.8%，至於中東煉油產能僅約 1120萬桶，占比約 10.9%為第四大煉油地區，美伊戰爭爆發
後，推估中東受損的煉油產能約 540 萬桶，占比約 5.2%。另一方面，烏俄戰爭演變為長期對抗，
烏克蘭無人機精準打擊俄羅斯煉油設備，俄羅斯煉油總產能約 550 萬桶，推估受損產能約 150 萬
桶，占比約 1.46%。若合計中東及俄羅斯受損的煉油產能，合計約 690萬桶，占比約 6.7%，全球
成品油供應受衝擊，也推動柴油及汽油價格與利差維持高檔。

### Page 9

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 10月 19日 FPCC 第9 頁，共 18頁  
亞洲廠商缺料減產的乙烯產能損失缺口約 16-17%，供需過剩格局趨於改善 
荷姆茲海峽封鎖期間，原油運輸中斷，高度依賴中東原料供應的亞洲煉油及石化廠商面臨缺料狀況，
包括日本、韓國、台灣、中國及東南亞等均有石化廠商相繼宣布不可抗力因素而減產，調降開工率。
以乙烯產能為例，亞洲乙烯產能佔全球比重約 45%，若因缺料而將開工率由原本正常的 75-80%水
準調降至 50%左右，推估一個月乙烯產能損失造成的乙烯供需缺口約 12.5%，加計中東約 15%乙
烯產能受損而運作不順(假設開工率僅 20%)，就算美洲(包括美國及南美)的石化廠商會伺機拉高開
工率填補，推估全球乙烯一個月實際的缺口約 16-17%。至於其他基礎石化原料因缺料及運作不順
的產能損失大概落在 14-17%不等。整體而言，缺料造成減產的供給限縮，有助於加速庫存去化，
塑化產品供需過剩格局趨於改善。

圖 9：全球石化基礎原料因缺料減產的產能損失推估比率彙整表

資料來源：元大投顧整理

中東受損的石化產能需要時間修復，有利石化產品價格及利差回穩 
美伊停火談判導致近 2026年 6月油價回落重挫，而下游產品買氣縮手，市場陷入觀望，拖累塑化
產品報價同步修正。美伊和談反覆，戰火再起，油價止跌回穩，不過荷姆茲海峽仍有油輪通行，加
上替代油路，原油供應逐漸恢復，亞洲石化廠商缺料緩解，開工率可望逐步提升，產出增加，另外
油價回升，有助於強化塑化產品報價的漲價預期，可止跌回穩。中長期而言，美伊戰爭引發的中東
局勢動盪可能往常態化發展，佔全球石化產能比重約 15%的中東地區，因戰爭受損運作停擺之產
能需要較長時間的修復，實質衝擊影響將擴大，預期供給端擴張幅度可控，有利塑化產品報價及利
差回穩，高於 2026年初美伊戰前水準，看法維持正向。

獲利調整與股票評價
煉油產能受損難以恢復，供應吃緊，利差展望佳，獲利上修
展望 2026 年，美伊戰爭引發的中東局勢動盪可能往常態化發展，中東產能運作受衝擊，且俄羅斯
煉油廠遭受烏克蘭攻擊，產出下滑，而歐美老舊煉油廠相繼關廠退出市場，中國亦有淘汰老舊煉油
廠產能規劃，供給受限，而美國恐限制柴油出口，成品油供給吃緊，而成品油需求穩健，維繫煉油
利差。台塑化獲利回溫，預估 2026 年營收上修 1.1%至 7826.12 億元(原估 7740.90 億元)，年增
25%，營益率上修 0.1ppt 至 13.1%(原估 13.0%)，營業利益上修 2.0%至 1025.35 億元(原估
1004.97億元)，年增 842.5%，稅後 EPS上修 1.2%至 9.12元(原估 9.01元)，年增 780.1%，年底
淨值 55.91元(原估 55.80元)。
展望 2027 年，OPEC+增產原油恐壓抑油價，不過中東局勢動盪常態化，加上 OECD 國家回補原

油庫存，將支撐油價，預期油價延續偏高檔整理，惟預期中東及俄羅斯受損的煉油產能可能逐步恢
復運作，煉油利差將較 2026 年滑落，但仍會優於美伊戰爭前的水準，預估 2027 年營收維持
7559.99億元，年減 3.4%，營益率上修 0.1ppt至 9.1%(原估 9.0%)，年減 4.0ppt，營業利益上修
0.8%至 684.10 億元(原估 678.60 億元)，年減 33.3%，業外方面，加計持有南亞(1303)及南亞科
(2408)預期將配發之現金股利合計推估約 72.7億元之挹注，稅後 EPS上修 10.9%至 6.84元(原估
6.17元)，年減 25.1%，年底淨值 56.36元(原估 55.66元)。若以 70%股利配發率計算，推估 2027
年台塑化將配息 6.39元，以目前股價計算，殖利率達 5.2%以上，仍具吸引力。
對台塑化看法正向，建議買進，目標價由 125元上調至 160元。考量：1)成品油供給吃緊，提振煉
油利差，3Q26營業利益 320.82億元，年增 245.6%，稅後 EPS為 2.76元，年增 206.3%，獲利

創單季新高。2) 沙烏地與葉門胡塞組織戰火擴大，油價高檔，而冬季取暖的柴油需求持續強勁，汽
油庫存偏低，中國限制出口，推動汽油價差擴大，4Q26營業利益上修 4.4%至 230.55億元，年增
338.4%，稅後 EPS 上修 4.2%至 2.04 元，年增 280.3%。預估 2026 年營業利益上修 2.0%至
1025.35億元，年增 842.5%，稅後 EPS上修 1.2%至 9.12元，年增 780.1%，擺脫 2025年獲利低
迷狀態。3)推估 2027年台塑化將配息 6.39元，以目前股價計算，殖利率達 5.2%以上，仍具吸引
力。4)受惠轉投資南亞科及南亞現金股利之貢獻，2027年稅後 EPS上修 10.9%至 6.84元。推估
台塑化 2027年底淨值增至 56.36元，目前本淨比 2.17倍，處於歷史本淨比 1.0倍-3.0倍的區間中
間，重申買進建議，目標價由 125元上調至 160元，本淨比由 2.2倍上修至 2.8倍及 2027年預估
每股淨值 56.36元推得，本淨比上修係反映煉油利差亮麗，提振獲利，評價上修。

公司簡介  
台塑石化股份有限公司(6505.TW)成立於 1992 年，由台塑、南亞、台化等公司共同出資成立，為國內唯一之民營石油煉製
業者，且石油製品及石化原料產能規模位居國內第一。另有合格汽電共生系統，供應台塑企業麥寮廠區各工廠所需之蒸汽、
電力等各項公用流體。公司主要業務分為油品及煉油、烯烴事業。油品銷售方面，主要以國內市場需求為優先， 2025 年銷
貨佔比為台灣 37%、歐洲 14%、新加坡 10%、英國 6%和其他 33%，汽柴油在國內市占率約 22%。塑化事業方面，乙烯
在國內市占率約 64%。主要客戶為台塑、台化、南亞、國喬、台橡、台達化等國內下游石化廠。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 台塑化整體的 ESG風險評級屬於嚴重風險，於 Sustainalytics資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於落後的
位置，亦於石油天然氣煉製行銷行業的公司中排名落後同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 台塑化的整體曝險較高，且略高於該石油天然氣煉製行銷行業的平均水準。在 ESG（環境、社
會與治理）方面，值得關注的實質議題包括：產品與服務、排放、廢水與廢棄物，以及職業健康與安全。  
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：台塑化在針對重大 ESG議題的管理水準和執行力為平均水準。該公司展現出堅定的
ESG 承諾，制定了完善的環境政策，並發布了符合國際永續發展標準的、高品質的 ESG 報告。公司透過董事會或高階
主管層面的委員會（或指定成員）強化了對 ESG 事務的監督。然而，其舉報人機制較為薄弱，顯示出該領域亟需改進。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

Document 249: Anthropic招股书里最值得看的14个细节

### Page 1

收⼊五个季度涨了约 30 倍，⼆季度已经转盈，上半年账⾯却亏了 807 亿美元，算⼒账单还
签到了 2045 年 ……Anthropic 招股书⾥最值得看的 14 个细节，不少都带着这样的反差。更
少⻅的是控制权：普通股东⼏乎没有话语权， 50.1% 的投票权握在七位创始⼈⼿⾥。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 26 ⽇， Anthropic 正式向美国证券交易委员会公开提交 IPO 申请，拟在纳斯达克挂牌，股票
代码 ANT ，⽬标估值 1.5 万亿美元⾄ 2 万亿美元，有望超越 SpaceX 创下的历史纪录。 IPO 时间窗
⼝据媒体爆料定于 11 ⽉ 16 ⽇当周。
当天，美股半导体板块应声上涨 —— 纳指盘中涨超 1% ，博通、 AMD 、英伟达各⾃拉涨近 3% 。
Anthropic 还未挂牌，最先涨的是它的供应商们。 Anthropic 已经签下总额超过 8000 亿美元的算⼒
合同，最⻓排到 2045 年。在市场看来，这些⻓单或许已经是写进硬件巨头资产负债表⾥的确定
性收⼊。
这份超 300 ⻚、约 24 万英⽂单词的 S-1 ，是公众第⼀次有机会完整审视这家世界顶级 AI 实验室的

财务底⾊与治理逻辑。英国《⾦融时报》将其定性为⼀场 “ 公投 ”—— 检验公开市场投资者究竟
有多⼤程度认同 Dario Amodei 以使命感征服私募⾦主的那套叙事。就⽬前市场情况来看，
Amodei 的叙事似乎反应不错。
叙事之外，招股书⾥的财务数据和细节同样引⼈注⽬。
收⼊上， 2025 年⼀季度 Anthropic 收⼊ 3.76 亿美元；今年⼆季度 115.55 亿美元，五个季度涨了约
30 倍。利润上，上半年净亏 807 亿美元，⼆季度经营利润却转正，达到 9.74 亿美元，差额主要来
⾃约 724 亿美元的公允价值变动，没有现⾦流出。控制权上，亚⻢逊、⾕歌持有的是没有投票权
的 N 类股，公司 50.1% 的投票权握在 1 股 F 类股⼿⾥，由七位联合创始⼈每⼈出资 1 美元认购。
同时， Anthropic ⼆季度到三季度收⼊增幅降⾄接近 50% ，前五⼤客⼾在前九个⽉贡献了四分之
⼀的收⼊。招股书 90 多⻚的⻛险因素⾥，公司写明技术可能带来 “ 灾难性或⽣存级⻛险 ” 。

⼀、疯狂的营收增速：五个季度涨了约 30 倍
增速有多夸张？ Anthropic S-1 ⾥⾃⼰给了数据对⽐。
招股书显⽰， 2023 年， Anthropic 实现营收 4100 万美元， 2024 年增⻓⾄ 3.86 亿美元， 2025 年进
⼀步达到 45.85 亿美元。 2026 年增⻓势头进⼀步加快，上半年公司实现营收 162.86 亿美元。按季
度看，2025 年⼀季度， Anthropic 收⼊ 3.76 亿美元；今年⼆季度， 115.55 亿美元，五个季度涨
了约 30 倍。中间四个季度依次是 7.87 亿、 13.82 亿、 20.40 亿、 47.31 亿。折合下来，⼆季度每天
约进账 1.27 亿美元。
截⾄今年前 9 个⽉，媒体披露，Anthropic 收⼊从去年的 46 亿美元增⾄今年前九个⽉的 330 亿美
元以上，过去⼀年⼈均收⼊ 660 万美元。
销售和市场费⽤同步跳升： 2025 年全年 5.68 亿美元，今年上半年就达到 31.68 亿美元。
不过，增速已有回落。今年⼀季度到⼆季度，收⼊翻了⼀倍多；⼆季度到三季度，增幅接近

50% 。 Anthropic 在招股书中提醒，迄今的快速增⻓ " 可能反映的是早期采⽤ " ，未来可能停滞。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⼆、半年亏 807 亿， 724 亿是纸⾯亏损
上半年净亏损 807.48 亿美元，看起来吓⼈，拆开利润表，其中 723.88 亿美元来⾃可转换票据、
衍⽣负债和 SAFE 的公允价值变动。
机制不复杂。亚⻢逊投给 Anthropic 的钱，有⼀部分是可转换票据，相当于⼀张写明 " 以后可按约
定价格换成股票 " 的借条。 Anthropic 的估值今年 2 ⽉是 3800 亿美元， 5 ⽉升到 9650 亿美元，借条
随之⼤幅增值，会计准则要求把增值部分记为公司的亏损。招股书也注明，这类变动主要由估
值上升推动，不涉及现⾦流出。
按公司给出的调整后（⾮ GAAP ）⼝径，上半年调整后营业利润为 16.18 亿美元，主要剔除了股
权激励及⾮现⾦慈善捐赠费⽤；调整后净利润为 26.71 亿美元
三、算⼒成本占收⼊⽐重：从 2.5 美元降到不⾜ 50 美分
据英国《⾦融时报》， 2025 年⼀季度， Anthropic 每 1 美元收⼊对应超过 2.5 美元的算⼒和基础设
施⽀出； 2026 年⼆季度，这⼀数字降到不⾜ 50 美分。

招股书给出的年度⼝径是： 2023 年算⼒⽀出超过收⼊的 10 倍， 2024 年为 653% ， 2025 年为
160% ， 2026 年上半年为 55% 。
利润随之转正。截⾄ 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇的三个⽉， Anthropic 经营利润率为 8% ，经营利润 10 亿美元，上年
同期亏损 12 亿美元。具体来看，⼆季度经营利润为 9.74 亿美元；加回股权激励等⾮现⾦⽀出
后，调整后经营利润为 22.35 亿美元，利润率 19% 。上半年经营活动现⾦流净流⼊ 39 亿美元。

### Page 3

招股书归纳的原因有四点：
训练效率提升。 Claude 越来越多地参与下⼀代模型的研发，公司内部称之为 "closing the
loop" （闭环）；
推理效率提升，同等能⼒所需的算⼒下降；
同时在亚⻢逊 Trainium 、⾕歌 TPU 、英伟达 GPU 和 AMD GPU 四类芯⽚上训练和运⾏模型，
并⾃建调度系统；
新模型降价。 2025 年 11 ⽉发布 Opus 4.5 后，价格和单任务成本下降， 2026 年⼀季度收⼊环⽐
增速从上⼀季度的 48% 升⾄ 132% ；今年 9 ⽉发布 Opus 5.5 和 Fable 5.1 ，单任务成本再降 25%
⾄ 45% 。
同时，公司警告业绩可能因算⼒⽀出出现 " 显著的期间波动 " 。
四、客⼾结构：五家客⼾占四分之⼀，但头部占⽐在下降
今年前九个⽉，五家客⼾贡献了 Anthropic 25% 的收⼊，年消费超过 1000 万美元的客⼾只有 250
家。
从季度趋势看，头部占⽐在下降。前五⼤客⼾的收⼊占⽐，从⼀季度的约 36% 降⾄三季度的约

21% ，三季度 79% 的收⼊来⾃前五⼤客⼾以外的群体，没有任何⼀家客⼾的占⽐超过 10% 。同
期，前五⼤客⼾的收⼊绝对值从约 17 亿美元升⾄约 35 亿美元。招股书还显⽰，约 48% 的收⼊来
⾃云服务商的市场渠道。
客⼾数量的变化：年消费 10 万美元以上的客⼾， 2023 年底约 20 家， 2025 年 9 ⽉约 950 家， 2026
年 9 ⽉超过 11900 家，另有约 4000 万个消费不到 10 万美元的客⼾。财富 500 强企业中，超过⼀半
已是年消费 10 万美元以上的客⼾，但这些企业在 Anthropic 上的花费，平均不到其总开⽀的
0.1% ，⽤得最多的前 10 家也不到 1% 。
净收⼊留存率约 900%，即⼀年前花 1 美元的客⼾，今年同期平均花 9 美元。这⼀数字不含订阅⽤
⼾。
收⼊类型上，按实际⽤量付费的收⼊ 2025 年为 38 亿美元，占 83% ， 2026 年上半年为 131 亿美
元，占 80% ；上半年订阅收⼊ 32 亿美元，上年同期为 1.8 亿美元。三季度超过 80% 的收⼊，来⾃

过去 6 个⽉内发布的模型版本。

### Page 4

五、 5% ⾄ 7% 的收⼊，来⾃后来被认定违规的客⼾
招股书披露，截⾄ 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇的前九个⽉， Anthropic 约有 5% ⾄ 7% 的收⼊，来⾃后来被认定违反使
⽤政策或商业条款的客⼾。原因在于从发现、调查到封号存在时间差，封号前已提供的服务，
收⼊照常确认。
公司同时提⽰， 8 ⽉和 9 ⽉加⼤了识别与封禁⼒度，此类收⼊占⽐预计会下降，也可能拉低未来
的收⼊和增速。以前九个⽉约 330 亿美元的收⼊粗算， 5% ⾄ 7% 对应约 16 亿⾄ 23 亿美元。
六、算⼒承诺：总额超过 8000 亿美元，最⻓排到 2045 年
Anthropic 把算⼒称为 " 关键投⼊ " ，已签下总额超过 8000 亿美元、最⻓延伸⾄ 2045 年的承诺。创
始⼈承认这些投⼊ " 乍看可能⼤得不负责任 " ，称⾃⼰的做法是 " ⼤胆但理智 " 。
主要合同包括：
博通：博通与⾕歌联合开发 TPU 并租给 Anthropic ，五年租约总额 1610 亿美元； 2028 年起，
Anthropic 预计在 TPU 上最多再花 1500 亿美元；

AMD ：预计⽀出超过 200 亿美元，另有期权可使规模⼤致翻倍， AMD 将随芯⽚交付向
Anthropic 投资最多 50 亿美元；
⾕歌、亚⻢逊、微软：未来⼗年⾄少提供 2694 亿美元云算⼒；
Nscale 、 Lambda ：合同⾦额最⾼ 860 亿美元。
三⼤云⼚商的期限各不相同：⾕歌⾄少 1145 亿美元，期限为 2026 年 4 ⽉⾄ 2033 年 7 ⽉；亚⻢逊⾄
少 1100 亿美元，⾄ 2036 年 4 ⽉；微软⾄少 449 亿美元，⾄ 2033 年 5 ⽉。三家合同均含 " 不论实际使
⽤量 " （ regardless of actual usage ）的付款条款，⾕歌和亚⻢逊的合同还约定⽤量不⾜时按差
额补⾜。这类 " 照付不议 " 条款在天然⽓、电⼒等能源⻓协中常⻅。
按招股书财务报表附注的⼝径汇总：截⾄ 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇，已签固定或保底采购承诺 2656 亿美元，已签
约尚未起算的数据中⼼与算⼒租约 3214 亿美元， 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇之后新签和修订的⻓期合同 2450 亿美

### Page 5

元，合计 8300 多亿美元。截⾄ 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇，公司持有现⾦及短期投资约 1316 亿美元，上半年通过 G
轮和 H 轮融⼊约 1104 亿美元。
成本与收⼊的节奏差异，也是投资者关注的焦点。招股书显⽰，约 80% 的收⼊按实际⽤量结
算，收⼊取决于客⼾调⽤量，⽽算⼒成本往往提前⼀年以上锁定。
七、每⽉ 31 亿美元付给⻢斯克
Anthropic 与 SpaceX 的合同总额最⾼ 1205 亿美元，期限到 2029 年。预计 12 ⽉算⼒全部上线后，
每⽉费⽤约 31 亿美元；从明年初起，任何⼀⽅可提前 90 天通知终⽌。
回头看这段关系，转折不少。今年 1 ⽉， Anthropic 切断了 xAI 员⼯通过 Cursor 使⽤ Claude 的权
限。 2 ⽉， Anthropic 宣布融资后，⻢斯克公开批评其 AI 。 5 ⽉， Anthropic 宣布租下 xAI 在孟菲斯
的 Colossus 1 数据中⼼，⻢斯克随后改⼝，称赞 Anthropic 的⼈能⼒强、在乎做正确的事。招股

书⾥的合同⽅是 SpaceX.AI LLC ，曾⽤名 X.AI LLC ，如今是 SpaceX 的全资⼦公司。
按 SpaceX 招股书，它整个 AI 业务今年⼀季度收⼊为 8.18 亿美元。 Anthropic 每⽉的算⼒费，约是
这个数字的近四倍。
⼋、亚⻢逊持股近五分之⼀，⾕歌持股约 7%
英国⾦融时报测算，假设 Anthropic 以 2 万亿美元估值上市，亚⻢逊在可转换票据全部转股后约持
股五分之⼀，价值约 4000 亿美元。它的持股上限是 12% ，超出部分以票据形式保留，随上限空
间释放逐步转股，但亚⻢逊可以豁免这⼀上限。
⾕歌持股约 7% ，并可能依据今年早些时候的协议再投资最多 200 亿美元，其中 100 亿美元可按发
⾏价认购；若双⽅同意，⾕歌也可作为 IPO 的基⽯投资者。
财务投资者⾥，⽂件显⽰ Spark Capital 投资不到 10 亿美元，持股价值 145 亿美元；据⼀位知情⼈
⼠， Coatue 投资 47.5 亿美元，持股规模与之相近。
解禁安排也不寻常。与 SpaceX 类似， Anthropic 上市后 76 天内设五个解禁⽇，⾮创始⼈董事、⾼

管和部分现任及前任员⼯在上市⾸⽇就能卖出⼀部分股票。通常早期投资者要等 IPO 后 180 天才
能减持。
员⼯那边，招股书按 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇完成上市做了模拟测算，激励费⽤合计约 297 亿美元，按约 5300 名
员⼯平均，每⼈⾄少 560 万美元；今年 4 ⽉的⼀次内部⽼股转让中，包括 Amodei 兄妹和 CFO 在内
的内部股东已套现 46.6 亿美元。
九、七个⼈各出 1 美元，换来 50.1% 的投票权
Anthropic 在招股书中承认，其 " 新颖的 " 治理结构与多数上市公司 " 差异显著 " ，普通股东对公司
决策⼏乎没有控制权。
媒体梳理的结构是：顶层是 " ⻓期利益信托 " （ LTBT ），成员⾃⾏任命，通常有权选举和罢免七名
董事中的四名； Dario Amodei 和 Daniela Amodei 兄妹不能被信托撤换；其余三名董事名义上由
股东选举，实际上由两⼈和另外五位联合创始⼈决定，因为他们通过⼀类特殊股票持有 50.1% 的
投票权。信托对第四个席位的控制，需要包括 Daniela Amodei 在内的提名委员会同意，出现分

歧时，由代表员⼯的⼀票打破僵局。

### Page 6

这类特殊股票的细节是：上市前，公司将向由七位联合创始⼈组成的 Founder LLC 发⾏唯⼀ 1 股
F 类股，该股票没有经济权益，投票权等于全部投票权的 50.1% ，包括选举 T 类董事之外的所有
董事。据招股书附件，七⼈每⼈向公司出资 1 美元，合计 7 美元，出资不可撤回。
反过来，亚⻢逊、⾕歌这样的⼤股东持有的 N 类股没有投票权。招股书还写明，公司属于纳斯达
克规则下的 " 受控公司 " ，可以豁免部分公司治理要求。
⼗、股价每涨 50% ，拿出最多 2% 做公益
上市前， Anthropic 已把 846,840,960 股 N 类股划⼊全资⼦公司 Anthropic 公益基⾦，占全⾯摊薄股
本的 5% 。
董事会还批准了⼀个框架：从 IPO 发⾏价起，股价每较上⼀个⾥程碑上涨 50% ，可以再向该基⾦
注⼊最多 2% 的股份。七位联合创始⼈各⾃承诺，把个⼈股权的 80% ⽤于公益。
⼗⼀、 90 多⻚⻛险因素：连保险业也兜不住
招股书的⻛险因素篇幅约为 SpaceX 同类披露的三倍，与企业常⻅的套话式披露⼤不相同。⽂件

警告，其技术可能对⼈类构成 " 灾难性或⽣存级⻛险 " ，并可能 " 为意图开发化学、⽣物或其他危
险武器的恶意⾏为者提供实质性帮助 " 。
⽂件描述的失控场景包括：模型未来可能出现 " 意外的 …… ⾃我保护⾏为 " ，⽐如试图抵制关机、
隐瞒或操纵信息，也可能⽣成类似 " 勒索 " 的胁迫性、欺骗性输出。⾄于这些⻛险有没有保险兜
底，招股书的说法是，没有任何单⼀保险公司，甚⾄整个保险业合计，也可能⽆⼒承保。
⻛险因素还提到，公司越来越多地⽤ AI 加速⾃⾝研发，可能使模型能⼒的提升快于安全措施的开
发和落实；公司过去为此推迟过模型发布，例⼦是通过 Project Glasswing 限量开放的 Mythos 。
政策层⾯，⽂件承认，美国政府可能抬⾼成本、限制业务或出台影响模型需求的新规。今年早
些时候， Anthropic 曾被美国国防部认定为 " 供应链⻛险 " ，政府机构合同收⼊约占公司收⼊的
1% 。招股书这部分的结语是： " 我们⾯临的许多⻛险相互关联，可能叠加或引发连锁的不利影

响。 "
⼗⼆、 15 亿美元的和解费
2025 年的利润表⾥，有⼀⾏很少⻅的科⽬叫 "Bartz settlement" ，⾦额 15 亿美元，对应作家
Andrea Bartz 等⼈起诉 Anthropic ⽤盗版书训练 Claude ⼀案。在 48 万多部符合条件的作品⾥，约
44 万部已被申领。法院在今年 7 ⽉ 20 ⽇给予终审批准。
⼗三、创始⼈的提醒
七位创始⼈在⽂件中劝潜在投资者 " 不要纯粹从交易⻆度看待你的购买 " ，还承认公司的决策 " 可
能与投资者预期背道⽽驰 " ，因为利润最⼤化要与 " 公共利益 " 权衡，⼀些商业押注 " 单独来看可能
显得奇怪或⾮常规 " 。媒体提到，创始团队强调⾃⼰仍在公司⾼层是 " 少⻅的 " ，并将其形容为对
OpenAI 的 " 隐晦⼀击 " ，后者已流失⼤部分创始团队。
另⼀个⼩细节在管理层章节： Dario Amodei 的履历列出了普林斯顿博⼠、斯坦福博⼠后、⾕歌
⼤脑和 OpenAI 的经历，没有提到他 2014 年底在百度跟随吴恩达做语⾳识别的那段。他本⼈曾

说，⾃⼰正是在百度第⼀次观察到数据越多、模型效果越稳定。
⼗四、代码 ANT ：员⼯⾃称 “Ants”

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.6
Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,
as we expected, though the mix highlights that the waning in
momentum is uneven, centred on investors rather than owner-
occupiers. Business credit meanwhile remains a source of
strength, notwithstanding some slowing recently.
For September, we expect credit growth to nudge to 0.5%mth,
with the divergence between housing credit and business
credit persisting.
AUS: Q3/Sep CPI (%qtr/%mth)

Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.6%, Westpac f/c: 1.3%,
Market f/c: 1.3%, Range: 0.6 to 1.4.
Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.4%, Westpac f/c: 0.6%,
Market f/c: 0.6 Range: 0.3 to 0.8
As noted in the review of the August CPI, our September
quarter CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr holding the
annual pace flat at 3.9%yr. At the margin there was a 0.04ppt
increase due to a lift in auto fuel but it still rounds to 1.3%.
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% highlighting that part

of the increase is due to usual seasonality.
In the month, the September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% with
the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Recreation & culture make
the largest contribution but transport is not far behind. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate increases 0.5%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.
AUS: Q3/Sep Trimmed Mean (%qtr/mth)
Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.9%,
Market f/c: 1.0%, Range: 0.9 to 1.0.

Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.3%,
Market f/c: 0.3%, Range: 0.2 to 0.4
Our September quarter Trimmed Mean estimate is unchanged
at 0.9%qtr with the annual pace dipping a touch from 3.6%yr
to 3.5%yr. There was a small downward revision of 0.03ppt to
0.88% compared to our original published estimate but it still
rounds to 0.9%qtr.
In the month of September we are looking for a 0.3% in
the Trimmed Mean holding the annual pace steady at 3.6%

which will be the fourth consecutive month at this pace. The
distribution of the September Trimmed Mean ranges from
–0.3% to 0.7%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation
picture will probably be judged to have worsened slightly
given recent movements in energy prices and the exchange
rate. A vote seems very possible as differences in view will
likely remain among the MPC about how much the medium-
term inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.
Forward guidance will likely leave open the option to raise

the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy Statement
without presenting it as a sure thing.
NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

Document 309: France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

2027 Budget: Fiscal consolidation must now resume

The 2027 budget, adopted by the French Council of Ministers today, calls for a reduction in France’s fiscal 
deficit to 5% of GDP in 2027 (down from 5.4% in 2026). This is a first step toward s the multi-year fiscal 
consolidation needed to stabilise the public debt-to-GDP ratio. This ratio is expected to rise further in 2027 
and until the deficit is reduced to 3% of GDP. Furthermore, given the increase in some expenditures (+0.6 pp 
of GDP for debt service and defence spending), the projected fiscal effort amounts to 1 pp. Nearly one-third 
of this effort would come from higher compulsory levies, and two -thirds from curbing government and 
social spending.

2027 Draft finance law: A step in the right direction. The government is forecasting an improvement in the fiscal 
deficit from 5.4% of GDP in 2026 to 5% in 2027. This projection is based on a credible growth assumption of 1% 
(see our analysis of French growth drivers). To achieve this target, compulsory levies (CL) are expected to rise to 
44.2% of GDP, an increase of 0.3 pp (the same increase as in 2026, compared with +0.9 pp in 2025). The public 
spending-to-GDP ratio would decline by 0.2 pp to 56.9%. The effort required to reduce the deficit to 5% is greater 
than it appears. Indeed, some expenditures will increase (interest payments by 0.4 pp and defence spending by 
0.2 pp). Consequently, the fiscal effort for next year amounts not to 0.4 pp, but to 1 pp of GDP. One-third of this will 
be achieved through increased CL, and two-thirds through spending cuts. Social spending will contribute, notably 
through the cancellation of EUR 3 billion in social contribution reductions for low-wage earners, as well as efforts 
totaling EUR 6 billion each in the areas of pensions and healthcare. Social spending shows the largest deviation 
from pre-COVID levels, with an increase representing 1.2 pp of GDP, nearly half of the gap between the 2026 
budget balance and a 3% deficit. Government spending, excluding interest payments and defence, is expected to 
be frozen in nominal terms.

A compromise seems likely despite a tight schedule. The French National Assembly will start reviewing the 
budget on Tuesday 13 October. The government will need to ensure that a majority vote against the draft budget 
does not form. The current circumstances, including increasing sovereign bond yields and the upcoming 
presidential election, call for a consensus based on fiscal consolidation of the size proposed by the government. If 
Parliament does not pass a budget by the end of the year, the government will have to choose between 
implementing the government’s budget proposal by executive order ( only possible if there was no parliamentary 
vote) or through a special law (as in 2025 and 2026). A special law would enable tax collection , while the 
government and social security agencies could continue to issue debt. In this case, the 2026 budget would apply 
in 2027 (without indexation) until Parliament passes a budget in the first few weeks of the year. This must occur 
before the end of February (when Parliament’s session ends, earlier than usual due to the presidential election).

The one-year postponement of the 5% budget deficit target has a measurable impact. The gap between the 
budget adopted for 2026 (deficit of 5% of GDP) and the actual budget outcome (deficit of 5.4%) is due to 
unforeseeable circumstances (the conflict in Iran  and extreme drought). However, postponing the deficit target  
previously set for 2026 to 2027 comes at a cost: 1) the public debt-to-GDP ratio is expected to reach 121% in 2027 
according to our forecasts (1 pp above our scenario with a 5% target in 2026 and a deficit reduced to 4.5% in 2027), 
and 2) the (upcoming) postponement to 2032 of the 3% of GDP deficit target (a commitment that most presidential 
candidates plan to make) rather than in 2029 (the government had committed to this with the European Commission 
in 2024). In 2032, the public debt-to-GDP ratio would stabilise at 124% of GDP (instead of 121.5% if the deficit had 
been reduced to 5% of GDP as early as 2026 and to 3% of GDP by the end of the decade).

Another delay in fiscal consolidation would further erode France’s credibility. This new delay could stem 
either from a decision by the government formed in May 2027 to immediately implement part of its agenda, or from 
the enactment of a special law that would remain in effect until (just after) the likely legislative elections in June  
2027  or  for  the  entire  year.  If  the  government  emerging  from  the  elections  were  to  resume  fiscal  
consolidation only in 2028 (with a deficit of 5% of GDP that it projects to reduce to 3% by 2032), public debt would 
reach 126% of GDP by that date (a one-year delay in fiscal consolidation would result in an additional 2 pp of 
debt). This further delay could potentially undermine the credibility of the next government, given that budget 
targets were  not met in three out of four years between 2023 and 2026, a situation that would be costly amid a 
sharp rise in long-term interest rates. Furthermore, a higher deficit would increase the amount of debt to be issued 
within an already  tight schedule. The Agence France Trésor (AFT) has announced debt issuances totaling EUR 
340 billion in 2027 (an  increase  of  EUR  20  billion  compared  to  2026).  This  increase  is  due  to  the  maturing  
COVID  related  debt (beginning in 2027 and continuing largely through 2030), while the amount of debt to be 
issued to finance the deficit will remain stable compared to 2026. A decrease from this last driver would be needed 
in subsequent years so that  the AFT’s debt issuance can stabilise.

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of the Advanced Economies Team, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Global air travel growth is set to rebound to 5% in 2027, but

intensify

elevated even if oil prices ease.

Jet fuel is often the largest or second-largest cost item for airlines.
In 2025, it accounted for 27% of total operational costs on average.
This share could move towards 35% in 2026 as a result of
persistently high jet fuel prices and elevated crack spreads, caused

Further geopolitical developments remain highly uncertain. Even if
the Middle East conﬂict is resolved and oil prices retreat, tight

reﬁnery capacity and depleted inventories are likely to keep middle-
distillate prices, including jet fuel, elevated well into 2027.

Fuel hedging provides less cushioning in
2027; fares must respond
Airlines employ very diﬀerent strategies to protect themselves
against fuel price shocks, using derivatives based on crude oil or jet
fuel. European airlines generally hedged 40-90% of their jet fuel
consumption, cushioning the initial impact of the price shock.
However, the Middle East conﬂict has lasted too long to avoid a

signiﬁcant impact. Existing hedges gradually roll oﬀ, increasing
exposure to prevailing market prices. Ryanair, which remains (80%)
hedged until the second quarter of 2027, has already warned of
signiﬁcantly higher fares.
Outside, the picture is mixed, but hedge ratios are generally lower,
and airlines in the US and China, for instance, don’t hedge at all.
This means that two airlines operating similar routes can face very
diﬀerent fuel bills.
Airline tickets in the US responded much

faster to the cost shock than in Europe

Fares already sharply higher in the US, less
so in Europe
The fuel price shock will increasingly feed through into higher fares.
US airlines’ practice of retaining relatively high exposure to fuel price
volatility resulted in an almost immediate pass-through, with ticket
prices rising solidly in the months after jet fuel prices started to rise.

It is also easier to raise prices when domestic competitors face a
similar cost shock.
The situation is diﬀerent in Europe, where hedging provided more
initial protection. This also means fares may still need to rise
signiﬁcantly if fuel prices fail to fall. Coverage gradually expires as
2027 progresses, leaving airlines increasingly exposed to higher
spot prices.

More factors are driving up costs,
particularly in Europe
Jet fuel is the clear outlier this year, but wage and maintenance
costs are also rising faster than the average price level. Borrowing
costs and aircraft leasing rates remain another factor, although the
impact depends on individual airlines’ strategies and ﬁnancial
structures.
European airlines also face rising costs from the European Union
Emissions Trading System (ETS) for ﬂights within the European

Economic Area. In addition, fuel suppliers are required to blend
sustainable aviation fuel (SAF) under ReFuelEU Aviation, starting at
2% and rising to 6% by 2030*. SAF still trades at a signiﬁcant
premium. This puts a price on emissions, and in combination, this
adds some $300 per tonne of jet fuel in 2026 (BNEF, ING Research).
Meanwhile, France and the Netherlands have either raised or plan
to raise ticket taxes, particularly on long-haul ﬂying, while Germany

has scaled its levy back and Sweden has abolished it.

### Page 10

Low-cost carriers are particularly exposed because fuel accounts
for a larger share of their cost base, and proﬁt bases are more
vulnerable. High hedge ratios initially cushion volatility, but these
hedges gradually roll oﬀ. The more challenging environment is
already visible in the performance of several European low-cost
carriers and will remain an important theme in 2027 if fuel prices
stay elevated.
Premium carriers have more room to manoeuvre. Demand for

premium seats (business, premium economy) is less price-
sensitive, providing airlines with greater scope to pass on higher
costs. Both United Airlines and Delta Airlines reported little impact
after price increases of 15-20% in the second to third quarters.
Passengers are booking later, but airlines continue to indicate a
strong propensity to ﬂy, especially in premium and premium-
economy classes. A minority also accounts for a large share of
ﬂights. In the Netherlands, for example, 25% of people account for

75% of journeys. These frequent travellers tend to be less price-
sensitive.
There has therefore been some demand destruction wiping out part
of the underlying growth. So far, though, the propensity to travel
has remained resilient.
Aviation remains proﬁtable, but margins hit
a post-pandemic low
The fuel shock has signiﬁcantly aﬀected margins. On average,
proﬁts in the global industry are expected to drop meaningfully
below 5%. Airlines including Ryanair, Southwest Airlines and AirAsia

have scaled back capacity on unproﬁtable routes as elevated fuel
prices persisted longer than expected.
According to global aviation consultancy Cirium, around 1% of
global air travel capacity will be removed heading into the Northern
Hemisphere winter of 2026-27. In less mature markets, maintaining
volumes while passing on the higher fuel bill is more diﬃcult.
Airlines may also prioritise less fuel-eﬃcient, older-generation
aircraft over capacity growth.

### Page 11

Industry headwinds could become a catalyst for further
consolidation, ranging from minority stakes to full takeovers.
Fares could retreat if fuel prices ease, but
don't expect too much
Dynamic and algorithm-based pricing is becoming increasingly
common. This helps airlines optimise already historically high load
factors (84% in 2026 so far) but can also contribute to greater fare
volatility, especially as passengers book later amid uncertainty.
If fuel prices ease in 2027, fares could drop back. Yet relatively

strong demand, constrained aircraft availability and general
inﬂation mean a return to early-2026 fare levels looks unlikely.
Cargo beneﬁts from high-tech demand and
supply-chain volatility
Air cargo continues to perform well as a fast transport option in an
increasingly volatile and less predictable world. Volume growth
remained almost 5% year-to-date, while freight rates remained
attractive for carriers.
While e-commerce drove much of the growth in recent years,

digitalisation and large-scale AI investment increasingly support
shipments of high-value technology products. Strong cargo
performance helped cushion pressure on airline results in 2026.
European carriers also beneﬁted from reduced available freight
capacity from Middle Eastern competitors.
Freighter capacity received a boost during the pandemic through
passenger-to-freighter conversions, but that boost has largely run
its course. New-generation A350 and Boeing 777 freighters should

add capacity in the coming years.
Aircargo volume resilient in disruptive
geopolitical environment

Future demand 2027 and onwards
Aviation’s centre of gravity is increasingly
shifting eastwards
The centre of gravity of global aviation is increasingly shifting
eastwards. Trips per capita are expected to see strong increases
over the next two decades, particularly in Asia. This matters even
more because these markets are far more populous.
Until recently, around 90% of the world’s population did not ﬂy in a
given year, but this is gradually changing. As a result, ﬂeet growth is

expected to be strongest in Asia, with its share of the global aircraft
ﬂeet increasing from around 33% to approximately 45%.
People still expected to ﬂy more, especially
in India

### Page 13

Source: S&P, Airbus, ING Research
India is becoming a powerhouse for future
airline growth
Air travel is strongly correlated with GDP and household income. As
Asia’s middle class expands, the potential customer base for air
travel swells. For future aviation demand, this income eﬀect is even
more important than population growth alone.
Younger generations also appear keen to travel, adding to the
potential in countries such as India and Indonesia. Increasingly

internationally dispersed families provide another structural source
of demand.
India stands out here. Strong economic growth, rising household
incomes and a huge population oﬀer strong potential. Airlines are
positioning themselves accordingly, illustrated by historically large
aircraft orders from IndiGo and Air India. India’s aircraft ﬂeet is
expected to grow by around 8% annually on average over the next
two decades.
China and India are the growth engines of

global aviation in the decades ahead

### Page 14

Source: S&P Global, Airbus, ING Research
Supply-side developments
Aircraft scarcity keeps capacity tight and
lease rates elevated
Supply constraints have been curbing aviation growth since the
post-pandemic recovery and remain a major factor. The share of
stored aircraft dropped below 10% by the end of 2025, its lowest
level since 2019. Even several A380s (the largest aircraft) have
returned to service.
More than half of the global commercial aircraft ﬂeet is leased.

Tight capacity is consequently reﬂected in relatively high used-
aircraft values and lease rates.
Airbus and Boeing had a combined record backlog of around
15,000 aircraft by mid-2026. Production and deliveries continue to
lag order intake after seven consecutive years of subdued deliveries
from 2019 onwards. Demand for new-generation aircraft remains
strong, as illustrated by major recent orders from Avolon and
Turkish Airlines.
Production could ﬁnally return to pre-pandemic levels in 2027.

Chinese manufacturer COMAC, meanwhile, is making progress in
China in Asia, but Western certiﬁcation remains distant.

### Page 15

New airplane deliveries continue on a
recovery track now with Boeing rebounding
Number of delivered commercial airplanes by manufacturers Airbus &
Boeing per year
Source: Company reports *Company forecast
Why aircraft supply remains constrained
Production has still not caught up with pre-pandemic levels
and is unlikely to do so in the next ﬁve years. Aircraft
manufacturing is recovering, but delays and large backlogs
push delivery dates into the 2030s. Airlines are therefore forced

to keep older aircraft ﬂying for longer.
Suppliers are still struggling to ramp up. Six years after the
sharp pandemic downturn, aviation supply chains have still
not fully normalised. Stringent safety and certiﬁcation
requirements mean manufacturers rely on a limited number of
specialised suppliers. Engines and sophisticated cabin
components still have long lead times, while skilled workers
remain scarce.
Engine issues continue to aﬀect available capacity. The peak

impact of the Pratt & Whitney inspection programme after an
identiﬁed production issue has passed, but checks and
maintenance continue to constrain parts of the A320neo-
family ﬂeet and absorb scarce maintenance capacity.

Source: Company reports
Delivery catch-up is crucial for both
capacity and efﬁciency
New aircraft are critical not only to accommodate growth, but also
to improve eﬃciency. The average age of the global ﬂeet has
increased from around 13 to 15 years since 2019 due to production
constraints.
This matters increasingly as older units require more maintenance

at a time when skilled personnel and maintenance capacity are
scarce; they also consume signiﬁcantly more fuel and therefore
emit more.
Boeing is ﬁnally regaining altitude
supporting deliveries
Boeing is ﬁnally making a sustained comeback following an
unprecedented period of disruption stemming from the 737 MAX
accidents, the pandemic and subsequent production and quality
problems. It received certiﬁcation for its 737 MAX 7 and also the
737 MAX 10 recently. Boeing plans to increase production for the

family from 38 to 47 a month.
